Classical solutions to the Boltzmann equations for gas mixture with unequal molecular masses Thanks:
Gaofeng Wang, Weike Wang and Tianfang Wu

Abstract
. ‌The Boltzmann equation is essential for gas thermodynamics, as it models how the molecular density distribution F ⁡ ( t , x , v ) F(t,x,v) changes over time. However, existing research primarily focuses on the single-species Boltzmann equation, while investigations into gas mixtures with unequal molecular masses remain relatively limited. Notably, mixed-gas studies have broader applications—exemplified by Earth’s atmosphere, composed of 78% nitrogen, 21% oxygen, and 1% trace gases, where the N 2 N_{2} to O 2 O_{2} molecular mass ratio is 28:32 (simplified as 7:8). This work addresses the Boltzmann equations for such mixtures with unequal molecular masses ( m A ≠ m B ) (m^{A}\neq m^{B}) , establishing the global-in-time existence of classical solutions near Maxwellians for soft potentials ( − 3 < γ < 0 -3<\gamma<0 ) in a periodic spatial domain. Our analysis encompasses arbitrary molecular mass ratios. ‌The main contribution of this paper lies in ‌the‌ detailed characterization of the linear collision operator’s structure and establishing estimates for the nonlinear terms under unequal mass conditions. ‌Consequently, these results‌ may help advance‌ spectral analysis for soft potentials ‌as well as L 2 − L ∞ L^{2}-L^{\infty} frameworks in future studies of multi-component Boltzmann equations. Keywords . Boltzmann equations; Gas mixtures; Unequal molecular masses; Global-in-time; Classical solutions; Soft potentials. AMS subject classifications: 35B38, 35J47



1.
Introduction and main result

1.1.
Introduction

We consider the Boltzmann equations for two species of particles

\left\{\begin{gathered}\partial_{t}F^{A}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F^{A}=Q^{AA}(F^{A},F^{A})+Q^{AB}(F^{A},F^{B}),\\
\partial_{t}F^{B}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F^{B}=Q^{BA}(F^{B},F^{A})+Q^{BB}(F^{B},F^{B}),\end{gathered}\right.

(1.1)

where the particle species is denoted by the Greek letters
\alpha,\beta\in\{A,B\}
.
F^{\alpha}(t,x,v)
is the spatially periodic density distribution function for the particles species
\alpha
at time
t\geq 0
, position
x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in[-\pi,\pi]^{3}=\mathbb{T}^{3}
, and velocity
v=(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Particle mass of species
\alpha
is denoted by
m^{\alpha}
.
The collision operator for
\alpha
-
\beta
particle pairs takes the form

Q^{\alpha\beta}(F^{\alpha},F^{\beta})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\Big[F^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})F^{\beta}(v^{\prime}_{*})-F^{\alpha}(v)F^{\beta}(v_{*})\Big]d\omega dv_{*}.

Here
-3<\gamma<0,\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
, and
b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)
satisfies the Grad angular cutoff assumption,

0<b^{\alpha\beta}(\cos\theta)\leq C_{b}|\cos\theta|,

where,
C_{b}
is a constant, and
\cos\theta=\frac{\omega\cdot(v-v_{*})}{|v-v_{*}|}
. Moreover, the velocities
v,v_{*}

and
v^{\prime},v^{\prime}_{*}
correspond to the species
\alpha
and
\beta
before and after the collision occurs, which obey the laws of momentum conservation and energy conservation:

m^{\alpha}v_{*}^{\prime}+m^{\beta}v^{\prime}=m^{\alpha}v_{*}+m^{\beta}v,\quad m^{\alpha}|v_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}+m^{\beta}|v^{\prime}|^{2}=m^{\alpha}|v_{*}|^{2}+m^{\beta}|v|^{2}.

So,
v^{\prime},v^{\prime}_{*}
can be expressed as

v^{\prime}=v-\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega,\qquad v^{\prime}_{*}=v_{*}+\frac{2m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega.

(1.2)

Over the past few decades, many mathematical theories have emerged regarding the Boltzmann equation, most of which focus on the single-species Boltzmann equation. Relatively few discussions address the Boltzmann equation for multi-species gases. For gas mixtures with equal molecular collision masses (
m^{A}=m^{B}
), H.L.Li, T.Yang and M.Y.Zhong
[
23
]
studied the decay rates of the bipolar Vlasov -Poisson-Boltzmann equations. Z.D.Fang and K.L.Qi
[
11
]
investigated the hydrodynamic transition process from Boltzmann equations to two-fluid systems. C.Bardos and X.F.Yang
[
4
]
gave the classification of well-posed kinetic boundary
layer for hard sphere gas mixtures. Meanwhile, Y.J.Wang
[
29
]
established the diffusive limit behavior of the VPB system in periodic domains under the same mass condition (
m^{A}=m^{B}
), and subsequently
[
30
]
provided rigorous analysis of its temporal decay properties. N.Jiang, Y.J.Lei, and H.J.Zhao
[
18
]
investigated the Euler-Maxwell limit for potential
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, (
m_{\pm}=e_{\pm}=1
).
However, when dealing with a mixed gas containing two species with different particle masses, more difficulties arise, primarily in three aspects:

(1)

The collision operator incorporates the mass parameters of different particles, complicating the analysis of its properties.

(2)

Collisions between particles of unequal mass lack symmetry.

(3)

Collision operator on the right-hand side cannot decouple the system of equations through
``(f^{+}+f^{-})^{\prime\prime}
and
``(f^{+}-f^{-})^{\prime\prime}
, which is different from the case of
m^{A}=m^{B}
.

Consequently, studies on collisions involving particles of different masses (
m^{A}\neq m^{B}
) are far fewer. K.Aoki, C.Bardos and S.Takata
[
1
]
investigated the Knudsen layer for gas mixtures, establishing that the equilibrium state for particles of unequal masses is a bi-Maxwellian distribution and proving that is a self-adjoint and non-negative operator.
In
[
28
]
, A.Sotirov and S.H.Yu investigate the Boltzmann equation for a binary gas mixture in one spatial dimension, with initial conditions where one gas is near vacuum and the other in proximity to a Maxwellian equilibrium state. They decompose the operator

\bm{L}(v)
into four scalar operators
L^{AA},L^{AB},L^{BA},L^{BB}
, where
L^{AA}
and
L^{BB}
share the same properties with the single-species operator
L
. Specifically, they establish the coercivity of the operator
L^{AB}
. Their work establishes a spectral analysis framework for asymmetric initial conditions, paving the way for subsequent research by other scholars—as demonstrated in
[
19
]
,
[
22
]
,
[
24
]
and
[
32
]
.

Later, Briant and Daus
[
7
]
studied the Boltzmann equation system for hard potentials with particles of different masses, employing spectral methods to construct operator semigroups and establish the well-posedness of solutions for small perturbations near equilibrium initial conditions. Their paper makes a contribution ‌by‌ providing mathematically rigorous treatment of gases composed of particles of unequal masses without structural constraints, ‌and by‌ establishing key properties of essential linear operators — ‌specifically‌ the coercivity of
\bm{L}(v)
and‌ decay estimates for
\bm{K}(v)
, thereby‌ providing a direct foundation for subsequent research
(
one can refer to
[
2
]
,
[
3
]
,
[
5
]
,
[
10
]
and
[
31
]
)
.

However, the conclusions and proofs ‌in
[
7
]
no longer apply for soft potentials. Specifically, Theorem 3.3’s method for proving coercivity of the linear collision operator
\bm{L}(v)
does not apply in case of
-3<\gamma<0
, as the spectrum of

\bm{L}(v)
no longer has a gap ‌near zero‌.
(
boldface denotes vector-valued operators
)
. Additionally, in
[
7
]
Lemma 5.1 they prove that
\bm{L}=-\bm{\nu}+\bm{K}
and the integral kernel
\bm{k}(v,v_{*})
of the
\bm{K}(v)
satisfies

\frac{\left\langle v\right\rangle^{l}}{\left\langle v_{*}\right\rangle^{l}}\big|\bm{k}(v,v_{*})\big|\leq C\big[|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}+|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma-2}\big]e^{-c(|v-v_{*}|^{2}+\frac{||v|^{2}-|v_{*}|^{2}|^{2}}{|v-v_{*}|^{2}})},

(1.3)

as well as in
[
7
]
Lemma 5.2:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\left\langle v\right\rangle^{l}}{\left\langle v_{*}\right\rangle^{l}}\big|\bm{k}(v,v_{*})\big|dv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big[|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}+|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma-2}\big]e^{-c(|v-v_{*}|^{2}+\frac{||v|^{2}-|v_{*}|^{2}|^{2}}{|v-v_{*}|^{2}})}dv_{*}\leq\frac{C}{1+|v|},

(1.4)

where
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
and
\left\langle v\right\rangle=(1+|v|),\,l\geq 0
.

Note that estimate (
1.3
) fails to provide any decay‌ about velocity
v
in
L^{\infty}_{v}
, because one can take
|v|
and
|v_{*}|
arbitrarily‌ large while satisfying

|v-v_{*}|=\big||v|^{2}-|v_{*}|^{2}\big|=1.

As shown in Figure 1:

Figure 1.
Why no decay in (
1.3
)

•

In Case 1,
\big||v|^{2}-|v_{*}|^{2}\big|=2\times 10^{4}+1
;

•

In Case 2,
\big||v|^{2}-|v_{*}|^{2}\big|=0
;

•

Thus‌, Case 3 exists where
\big||v|^{2}-|v_{*}|^{2}\big|=1
.

Moreover, when
-3<\gamma\leq-1
, estimate (
1.4
) ‌is not integrable‌. This estimate ‌is also likely crude for the hard potentials case‌, ‌as‌ its decay rate
\frac{C}{1+|v|}
, does not reflect‌ the dependence on the potential parameter
\gamma
.

For the Boltzmann equation of a single species of particles, in fact, the corresponding integral kernel
k(v,v_{*})
satisfies the following improved decay estimate:

\displaystyle\frac{\left\langle v\right\rangle^{l}}{\left\langle v_{*}\right\rangle^{l}}\big|k(v,v_{*})\big|\leq C\frac{(1+|v|+|v_{*}|)^{\gamma-1}}{|v-v_{*}|}\,e^{-c\{|v-v_{*}|^{2}+\frac{||v|^{2}-|v_{*}|^{2}|^{2}}{|v-v_{*}|^{2}}\}},\hskip 14.22636pt-1<\gamma\leq 1,

(1.5)

\displaystyle\frac{\left\langle v\right\rangle^{l}}{\left\langle v_{*}\right\rangle^{l}}\big|k^{\chi}(v,v_{*})\big|\leq C_{\varepsilon}\frac{(1+|v|+|v_{*}|)^{\gamma-1}}{|v-v_{*}|}\,e^{-c\{|v-v_{*}|^{2}+\frac{||v|^{2}-|v_{*}|^{2}|^{2}}{|v-v_{*}|^{2}}\}},\hskip 14.22636pt-3<\gamma\leq-1,

(1.6)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\left\langle v\right\rangle^{l}}{\left\langle v_{*}\right\rangle^{l}}\big|k(v,v_{*})\big|dv_{*}\leq C\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\gamma-2},\hskip 14.22636pt-1<\gamma\leq 1,

(1.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\left\langle v\right\rangle^{l}}{\left\langle v_{*}\right\rangle^{l}}\big|k^{\chi}(v,v_{*})\big|dv_{*}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\gamma-2},\hskip 14.22636pt-3<\gamma\leq-1,

(1.8)

where
\chi=\chi_{\varepsilon}
is a cutoff function defined in (
2.10
) and
k^{\chi}(v,v_{*})
is the kernel of
K^{\chi}(v)
, defined as the regular part of
K(v)
. Estimates (
1.5
) and (
1.7
) (see
[
14
]
,
[
15
]
), ‌exhibit‌ a decay of
(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}
. (
1.6
) and (
1.8
), proved in
[
13
]
and
[
20
]
, provide finer estimates than (
1.4
).‌ These finer decay analyses are useful, ‌especially‌ when dealing with problems of spectral analysis for soft potentials, one can refer to
[
9
]
,
[
23
]
,
[
25
]
. They are also crucial for
L^{\infty}
estimates along the characteristics within the
L^{2}-L^{\infty}
framework (see
[
16
]
,
[
17
]
,
[
21
]
,
[
26
]
), where any slight decay shortfall would prevent the energy estimate from closing.

‌‌Since these refined estimates are critically important, it naturally raises the question: Does the corresponding integral kernel

\bm{k}(v,v_{*})
for multi-particle systems similarly satisfy such estimates? Therefore, a more detailed structural dissection of the linear operator
\bm{K}
is necessary. ‌Here we focus on the case of soft potentials.
\bm{K}
is decomposed into
\bm{K}_{1}+\bm{K}_{2}
. The structure of operator
\bm{K}_{1}
is relatively simple. Using momentum and energy conservation in collisions establishes its exponential decay, thus satisfying estimates (
1.6
),(
1.8
). For the part of
\bm{K}_{2}
, it is decomposed via a cutoff function
\chi=\chi_{\varepsilon}
, as
\bm{K}_{2}=\bm{K}^{\chi}_{2}+\bm{K}^{1-\chi}_{2}
. If
\varepsilon
is chosen sufficiently small,
\bm{K}^{1-\chi}_{2}
becomes small that can always be absorbed by the right-hand side. ‌Let
\bm{f}=(f^{A},f^{B})^{T}
be a vector valued function. The part of
\bm{K}_{2}^{\chi}\mathbf{f}=(K_{2}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f},K_{2}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f})^{T}
requires focused analysis. It admits the following decomposition:

K_{2}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}=\underbrace{K_{2,(1)}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}}_{\text{Typical part}}+\underbrace{K_{2,(2)}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}}_{\text{Hybrid part}},

Figure 2.
Decomposition of the Linear Operator
\mathbf{K}^{\chi}

where

\begin{split}&K_{2}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\chi_{\varepsilon}(v-v_{*})\Big[\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}f^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 219.08612pt+\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\,f^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\Big]b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)d\omega dv_{*}.\end{split}

Both parts
\alpha\neq\beta
and
\alpha=\beta
in “Typical part” exhibit properties analogous to ‌those in the Boltzmann equation for a single species, which is equivalent to the case of identical molecules
(m_{A}=m_{B})
. So, its integral kernel
k_{\alpha\beta}^{(1)}(v,u_{\parallel})
thus fulfills estimates (
1.6
),(
1.8
). “Hybrid part” comprises a ‌degenerate part
(m^{\alpha}=m^{\beta})
and an ‌exponentially decaying part
(m^{\alpha}\neq m^{\beta})
. ‌It is straightforward to verify that the integral kernel
k_{\alpha\beta}^{(2)}(v,u_{\perp},u_{\parallel})
of the exponentially decaying part satisfies estimates (
1.6
),(
1.8
). While the degenerate part exhibits symmetry with the
\alpha=\beta
part in “Typical part”,
(
thus maintaining the same structure to that in equal-mass collisions
)
, its integral kernel
k_{\alpha\alpha}^{(2)}(v,u_{\perp})
also fulfills these improved estimates. Details can be found in Lemma
2.2
and Remark
2.1
. How to formally transform these integral kernels to be consistent with (
1.6
),(
1.8
), see Remark
2.2
.

For hard potentials, we can drop the cutoff function
\chi_{\varepsilon}
while preserving the above conclusions. These notations here carry a subtle distinction from that in the proof of Lemma
2.2
, primarily due to the inclusion of a truncation function
\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}
introduced to simultaneously establish operator compactness in Lemma
2.2
. ‌Yet, this adjustment sustains the principal properties of the decay-related decomposition. Therefore, the answer is ‌Yes!‌

Based on the structural analysis of the linear operator
\bm{K}
, we can further derive the coercivity for the linear operator
\bm{L}
. ‌The proof we employ ‌not only‌ establishes the result for soft potentials ‌but also‌ applies to hard potentials, because ‌when
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
,
\bm{K}
is a compact operator. Thus differs from the proof in
[
7
]
. In a subsequent section, we provide some estimates for the nonlinear term
\Gamma^{\alpha\beta}
. Although these nonlinear collision terms involve parameters
m^{\alpha}
,
m^{\beta}
and couple functions
f^{\alpha}_{1}
and
f^{\beta}_{2}
together, we achieve the desired control by considering their vector-valued weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
norms. ‌Furthermore‌, ‌to extend the existence time of local solutions to long time, we ‌generalize‌ Guo’s method
(
see Lemma 9 in
[
13
]
)
to the case of collisions with unequal masses and prove that the coercivity of the linear operator
\bm{L}
is ‌preserved under integration with respect to any large time t‌. Utilizing the properties of these operators and established lemmas, we finally prove the main result—Theorem
1.1
, establishing the global-in-time existence of classical solutions near Maxwellians for soft potentials (
-3<\gamma<0
).

1.2.
Notations

We use notation
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}
to denote the norm of
L^{p}
(
1\leq p\leq\infty
) space with respect to the variables
(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and
|\cdot|_{L^{p}_{v}}
,
|\cdot|_{L^{p}_{x}}
are the
L^{p}
norms w.r.t. the variable
v
or
x
. For vector functions
\mathbf{f}(t,x,v)=(f^{A},f^{B})^{T}
, its
L^{p}
norms are denoted by

\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}=\|f^{A}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\|f^{B}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}},\qquad|\mathbf{f}|_{L^{p}_{v}}=|f^{A}|_{L^{p}_{v}}+|f^{B}|_{L^{p}_{v}}.

The notations
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
and
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
are the inner product of the function space
L^{2}_{x,v}
and
L^{2}_{v}
respectively. For vector functions
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{g}(t,x,v)=(g^{A},g^{B})^{T}
, their inner product in
L^{2}_{v}
and
L^{2}_{x,v}
are denoted as

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\left\langle f^{A},g^{A}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\left\langle f^{B},g^{B}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=\left\langle f^{A},g^{A}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\left\langle f^{B},g^{B}\right\rangle_{{L^{2}_{x,v}}}.

We introduce the matrix product of column vectors
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{g}
:

(\mathbf{f}:\mathbf{g})=(f^{A}g^{A},f^{B}g^{B})^{T}.

The letter
C
is a constant, which may vary from line to line.
J\lesssim K
means
J\leq CK
. If both hold
J\lesssim K
and
K\lesssim J
, then we use the notation
J\thicksim K
.
We define the polynomial weight function
\left\langle v\right\rangle=1+|v|
, and
w=w(v)=(1+|v|)^{\gamma}
. Then we introduce the following weighted
L^{2}
norm

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\nu}^{2}=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f|^{2}w\,dxdv,\qquad|f|_{\nu}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f|^{2}w\,dv.

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\nu}^{2}=\|f^{A}\|_{\nu}^{2}+\|f^{B}\|_{\nu}^{2},\quad\quad\quad|\mathbf{f}|_{\nu}^{2}=|f^{A}|_{\nu}^{2}+|f^{B}|_{\nu}^{2}.

Let
\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\beta}
denote multi-indices with length
|\tilde{\alpha}|
and
|\tilde{\beta}|
respectively, and

\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\tilde{\beta}}\equiv\partial_{x}^{\tilde{\alpha}}\partial_{v}^{\tilde{\beta}}.

Furthermore, we denote
\tilde{\beta}\leq\tilde{\alpha}
if no component of
\tilde{\beta}
is greater than the corresponding component of
\tilde{\alpha}
, and
\tilde{\beta}\leq\tilde{\alpha}
if
\tilde{\beta}\leq\alpha
and
|\tilde{\beta}|<|\alpha|
, and use

C^{\tilde{\beta}}_{\tilde{\alpha}}
to denote the combination number.

Global solutions are constructed in the following energy norm:

\mathcal{E}[\mathbf{f}(t,x,v)]=\sum_{|\tilde{\alpha}|+|\tilde{\beta}|\leq N}\left(\frac{1}{2}\big\|w^{|\tilde{\beta}|}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}^{\tilde{\alpha}}\mathbf{f}(t)\big\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big\|w^{|\tilde{\beta}|}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}^{\tilde{\alpha}}\mathbf{f}(s)\big\|^{2}_{\nu}\,ds\right)

(1.9)

with the initial data satisfies

\mathcal{E}[\mathbf{f}^{\rm{in}}]\equiv\mathcal{E}[\mathbf{f}(0)]\equiv\sum_{|\tilde{\alpha}|+|\tilde{\beta}|\leq N}\frac{1}{2}\big\|w^{|\tilde{\beta}|}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}^{\tilde{\alpha}}\mathbf{f}_{0}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}.

Throughout this article,
N\geq 8
, and the Einstein summation convention is used
from time to time.

1.3.
Basic properties of collision operators
[
6
]
.

We introduce the bilinear Boltzmann collision operator
\mathscr{C}
in vector form:

\mathscr{C}\mathbf{F}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}Q^{AA}(F^{A},F^{A})+Q^{AB}(F^{A},F^{B})\\
Q^{BA}(F^{B},F^{A})+Q^{BB}(F^{B},F^{B})\end{array}\right).

(1.10)

Firstly, we list some properties of the collision operators.

(Collision invariants)
\mathbf{\Psi}=(\Psi^{A},\Psi^{B})^{T}
is called a collision invariant of operator
\mathscr{C}
with the inner product of
(L^{2}_{v}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))^{2}
if it satisfies

\langle\mathscr{C}\mathbf{F},~~\mathbf{\Psi}\rangle_{(L_{v}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))^{2}}=\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in\{A,B\}}\left\langle Q^{\alpha\beta}(F^{\alpha},F^{\beta}),~~\Psi^{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}=0\quad{\rm for~any~vector}\,~\mathbf{F}.

(1.11)

If and only if
\mathbf{\Psi}
belongs to
{\rm Span}\big\{\mathbf{e}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{2},v_{1}\mathbf{m},v_{2}\mathbf{m},v_{3}\mathbf{m},|v|^{2}\mathbf{m}\big\}
, where
\mathbf{m}=(m^{A},m^{B})^{T}
, and
\mathbf{e}_{j}
is the
j^{th}
unit vector in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

(H-theorem)
The entropy function of the two-species Boltzmann equations satisfies

\left\langle\mathscr{C}\mathbf{F},\log\mathbf{F}\right\rangle_{(L_{v}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))^{2}}=\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in\{A,B\}}\left\langle Q^{\alpha\beta}(F^{\alpha},F^{\beta}),~~\log F^{\alpha}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq 0,

(1.12)

and this equality holds if and only if

\log\mathbf{F}\in{\rm Span}\big\{\mathbf{e}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{2},v_{1}\mathbf{m},v_{2}\mathbf{m},v_{3}\mathbf{m},|v|^{2}\mathbf{m}\big\}.

(1.13)

In this article, we study the initial value problem

(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\mathbf{F}=\mathscr{C}\mathbf{F},\quad\mathbf{F}(0,x,v)=\mathbf{F}_{0}(x,v),

(1.14)

in a periodic box From the property of the collision invariants , the conservation of mass, momentum and energy for (
1.14
) take the form

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left\langle\mathbf{F}(t),\mathbf{e}_{i}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}dx

\displaystyle\equiv 0,\quad i=1,2,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left\langle\mathbf{F}(t),v_{j}\mathbf{m}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}dx

\displaystyle\equiv 0,\quad j=1,2,3,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left\langle\mathbf{F}(t),\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}\mathbf{m}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}dx

\displaystyle\equiv 0.

is to construct classical solutions for (
1.14
) near a global bi-Maxwellian

\displaystyle\bm{\mu}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mu^{A}\\
\mu^{B}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\frac{({m^{A}})^{3/2}}{(2\pi)^{3/2}}e^{-\frac{m_{A}|v|^{2}}{2}}\\
\frac{({m^{B}})^{3/2}}{(2\pi)^{3/2}}e^{-\frac{m_{B}|v|^{2}}{2}}\end{array}\right).

We denote

\sqrt{\bm{\mu}}=(\sqrt{\mu^{A}},\sqrt{\mu^{B}})^{T},\hskip 28.45274ptm^{s}=\min\big\{m^{A},m^{B}\big\},\hskip 28.45274pt\mu^{s}=\frac{({m^{s}})^{3/2}}{(2\pi)^{3/2}}e^{-\frac{m_{s}|v|^{2}}{2}}.

(1.19)

The ‌orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{3}(v)
is defined
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{\chi}}_{1}=\kappa_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1}:\bm{\mu}),\quad\tilde{\bm{\chi}}_{2}=\kappa_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{2}:\bm{\mu}),\quad\tilde{\bm{\chi}}_{j}=\kappa_{j}v_{j-2}(\mathbf{m}:\bm{\mu}),\,\,j=3,4,5,\quad\tilde{\bm{\chi}}_{6}=\kappa_{6}|v|^{2}(\mathbf{m}:\bm{\mu}).

(1.20)

where
\kappa_{i}
are some constants such that
\|\tilde{\bm{\chi}}_{i}\|_{L_{v}^{2}}=1
.

\mathbf{L}
is a non-negative operator, and
\left\langle\mathbf{L}\mathbf{f},\mathbf{f}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}=0
if and only if

\mathbf{f}=\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{f}

(1.21)

where the projection
\mathbf{P}_{0}
in
L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
is defined as

\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{f}(t,x,v)=\sum^{6}_{j=1}\big\langle\mathbf{f}(t,x,\cdot),\tilde{\bm{\chi}}_{j}\big\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}\tilde{\bm{\chi}}_{j}.

The perturbation function
\mathbf{f}=(f^{A},f^{B})^{T}
to
\bm{\mu}
is defined by

\mathbf{F}=\bm{\mu}+(\sqrt{\bm{\mu}}:\mathbf{f})

(1.22)

then the Boltzmann equation (
1.14
) can be written as

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\mathbf{f}+\mathbf{L}\mathbf{f}=\bf{\Gamma}(f,f),\\
\mathbf{f}(0,x,v)=\mathbf{f}^{\rm{in}}(x,v)\end{split}

(1.23)

Here the standard linear operator
\mathbf{L}=(L^{A},L^{B})^{T}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}L^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\left[Q^{\alpha\beta}(\mu^{\alpha},\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}}f^{\beta})+Q^{\alpha\beta}(\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}f^{\alpha},\mu^{\beta})\right],\end{split}

(1.24)

which can be decomposed by
\mathbf{L}\mathbf{f}=(\bm{\nu}:\mathbf{f})+\mathbf{K}\mathbf{f}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{f}=\left(\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathbf{L}^{A}\mathbf{f}\\
\mathbf{L}^{B}\mathbf{f}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{lll}\upsilon^{A}f^{A}\\
\upsilon^{B}f^{B}\end{array}\right)+\left(\begin{array}[]{lll}K^{A}\mathbf{f}\\
K^{B}\mathbf{f}\end{array}\right),\end{split}

(1.25)

where

\begin{split}&\upsilon^{\alpha}=\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})d\omega dv_{*},\quad K^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=K_{1}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}+K_{2}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f},\\
&K_{1}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu^{\alpha}(v)\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}f^{\beta}(v_{*})d\omega dv_{*},\\
&K_{2}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\Big[\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}f^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 193.47882pt+\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\,f^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\Big]d\omega dv_{*}.\end{split}

(1.26)

Moreover, the non-symmetric bi-linear form

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bf{\Gamma}(f,f)=\left(\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle{\sum_{\beta=A,B}}\Gamma^{A\beta}(f^{\alpha},f^{\beta})\\
\displaystyle{\sum_{\beta=A,B}}\Gamma^{B\beta}(f^{\alpha},f^{\beta})\end{array}\right),\end{split}

(1.27)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Gamma^{\alpha\beta}(f^{\alpha},f^{\beta})=&\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}Q^{\alpha\beta}(\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}f^{\alpha},\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}}f^{\beta})\\
=&\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)\\
&\times\left(\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}f^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})f^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})-\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v)\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}f^{\alpha}(v)f^{\beta}(v_{*})\right)d\omega dv_{*}\\
=:&\Gamma^{\alpha\beta}_{\rm gain}-\Gamma^{\alpha\beta}_{\rm loss}.\end{split}

(1.28)

In this paper, we suppose the initial data
\mathbf{F}^{\rm{in}}(x,v)
has the same mass, momentum and energy as the bi-Maxwellian
\bm{\mu}
, then for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\big\langle\mathbf{f},(\mathbf{e}_{i}:\sqrt{\bm{\mu}})\big\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}dx

\displaystyle=0,\quad i=1,2,

(1.29)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\big\langle\mathbf{f},(v_{j}\mathbf{m}:\sqrt{\bm{\mu}})\big\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}dx

\displaystyle=0,\quad j=1,2,3,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\big\langle\mathbf{f},(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}\mathbf{m}:\sqrt{\bm{\mu}})\big\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}dx

\displaystyle=0.

1.4.
The main result

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
-3<\gamma<0
, and
\mathbf{f}^{\rm{in}}(x,v)
satisfies the conservation law (
1.29
) at
t=0
with

\mathbf{F}^{\rm{in}}=\bm{\mu}^{\rm{in}}+(\sqrt{\bm{\mu}^{\rm{in}}}:\mathbf{f}^{\rm{in}})

There are constants
C_{0}>0
and
\varepsilon_{0}>0
, such that if
\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{f}^{\rm{in}})\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, there exists a unique global solution
\mathbf{f}(t,x,v)
to the Boltzmann equation (
1.23
) with
\mathbf{F}=\bm{\mu}+(\sqrt{\bm{\mu}}:\mathbf{f})
, and

\sup_{0\leq s\leq\infty}\mathcal{E}\big[\mathbf{f}(s)\big]\leq C_{0}\mathcal{E}(\mathbf{f}^{\rm{in}}).

Remark 1.1
.

‌This work builds on a energy method developed by Guo Yan in
[
12
]
,
[
13
]
to establish global classical solutions.
Despite the complexity of collisions between particles of differing masses and the limited mathematical literature available, examining each component in isolation leads to confusion and inconclusive results. However, adopting a holistic perspective on these collision operators reveals a key insight: by replacing the
space with a ‌2D vector-valued
space‌, the system as a whole satisfies conclusions analogous to those of the single-species Boltzmann equation.

Figure 3.
Structure presentation of the paper

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
-3<\gamma<0
and assume that

\mathbf{f}^{\rm{in}}(x,v)
satisfies the conservation law (
1.29
) at
t=0
. Let
\mathbf{f}(t,x,v)
be the classical solution to the Boltzmann
equation (
1.23
) in
[0,T^{*})
such that for
0\leq t<T^{*}
,

\mathcal{E}\big[\mathbf{f}(t)\big]\leq M.

Then there exists a small constant
0<\delta_{M}<1
, such that for any
t_{1}\geq 0
, and any
non-negative integer
n
with
0\leq t_{1}+n<T^{*}
,

\sum_{|\tilde{\alpha}|\leq N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+n}\big\langle\mathbf{L}\big[\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}\mathbf{f}(s)\big],\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}\mathbf{f}(s)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\,ds\leq\delta_{M}\sum_{|\tilde{\alpha}|\leq N}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+n}\|\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}\mathbf{f}(s)\|^{2}_{\nu}\,ds.

Remark 1.2
.

‌The proof of this theorem resides primarily in Lemma
5.1
, which is proved by contradiction. If (
5.1
) were false, we obtain a normalized sequence
\mathbf{Z}_{n}=(Z^{A}_{n},Z^{B}_{n})^{T}
satisfy (
5.3
) and (
5.4
). ‌The proof then proceeds in two steps:
‌The first step is to prove that the limit function takes the form:

\mathbf{Z}(t,x,v)=(\mathbf{a}(t,x):\sqrt{\bm{\mu}})+(\mathbf{b}(t,x)\cdot v)(\mathbf{m}:\sqrt{\bm{\mu}})+c(t,x)|v|^{2}(\mathbf{m}:\sqrt{\bm{\mu}}).

‌The second step demonstrates that

\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b},c
satisfy

\sum_{\tilde{\alpha}\neq 0,|\tilde{\alpha}|\leq N}\int_{0}^{1}\left[\big|\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}\mathbf{a}\big|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\big|\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}\mathbf{b}\big|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\big|\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}c\big|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\right](s)ds\leq CM,

where
\mathbf{a}=(a_{1},a_{2}),\,\mathbf{b}=(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})
. In the first step, ‌it is essential to treat the vector-valued function
\mathbf{Z}
as an integrated entity‌, since these lemmas such as Lemma
2.1
—
3.1
, hold for the vector as a whole but not for its individual components. In the second step, ‌component-wise estimation of
Z^{\alpha}
becomes feasible‌ since the limit function
\mathbf{Z}
has been proven to satisfy equation (
5.19
) in the sense of distributions. Specifically,
Z^{A}

satisfies equations related to
(a_{1},\mathbf{b},c)
, while
Z^{B}

corresponds to
(a_{2},\mathbf{b},c)
. The coefficients in both equations can be estimated separately.

The paper is structured as follows
.
Section 2‌ provides a detailed discussion of the decomposition of the linear collision operator ‌
\bm{L}

(m^{A}\neq m^{B})
, which is further used to prove the coercivity. ‌Section 3‌ gives estimates for the nonlinear collision operator ‌
\bm{\Gamma}
. ‌Section 4‌ obtains a local-in-time solution to the Boltzmann equation by constructing an iterative sequence. ‌Section 5 proves the coercivity involving time (see (
5.1
)) under the amplitude assumption (
4.11
), utilizing the conservation law equations. Finally, in ‌Section 6‌, by combining the local existence of the solution established in ‌Section 4‌ and the key inequality (
5.1
) derived in ‌Section 5‌, we prove the global existence of the solution.

2.
The linear operator
\mathbf{L}

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
\mathbf{f}_{i}=(f^{A}_{i},f^{B}_{i})^{T},(i=1,2)
be two vector functions, and
\mathbf{K}
be defined in (
1.25
). For
\theta\geq 0
,

\big|\big\langle w^{2l}\mathbf{K}\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big|\leq C|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}|_{\nu}|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}|_{\nu}.

(2.1)

Proof.

By the decomposition
K^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=K_{1}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}+K_{2}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}
, one has

\langle w^{2l}\mathbf{K}\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\langle w^{2l}\mathbf{K}_{1}\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\langle w^{2l}\mathbf{K}_{2}\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}.

For
\mathbf{K}_{1}
part, integrating
\omega
over
S^{2}
yields

\displaystyle\big\langle w^{2l}\mathbf{K}_{1}\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha=A,B}\sum_{\beta=A,B}C_{\alpha\beta}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v)}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*})f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)\,dv_{*}\,dv

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\alpha=A,B}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(v)\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v)}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}[f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*})]^{2}\,dv_{*}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(v)\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v)}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}\,dv_{*}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}.

Since
w(v)=(1+|v|)^{\gamma}
and
\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}
decays exponentially, we integrate the first factor of
f^{\beta}_{1}
over
v
, and integrate the second factor of
f^{\alpha}_{2}
over
v_{*}
, to obtain

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle w^{2l}\mathbf{K}_{1}\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\alpha=A,B}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w^{2l+1}(v_{*})[f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*})]^{2}\,dv_{*}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w^{2l+1}(v)[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}\,dv\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}|_{\nu}|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}|_{\nu},

(2.2)

which concludes the
\mathbf{K}_{1}
part.

By the law of energy conservation,
K_{2}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}
defined in (
1.26
) can be written as

\begin{split}&K_{2}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=-\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\Big[\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}f^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 184.9429pt+\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v_{*})}\,f^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\Big]d\omega dv_{*}.\end{split}

Then, for the part of
\mathbf{K}_{2}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\big|\big\langle w^{2l}\mathbf{K}_{2}\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big|\\
&\hskip 22.76228pt\lesssim\sum_{\alpha=A,B}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v_{*})}\,f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)dv_{*}\,dv\\
&\hskip 96.73918pt+\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)dv_{*}\,dv\Bigg).\end{aligned}

(2.3)

The first term inside the summation symbol in (
2.3
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v_{*})}\,[f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})]^{2}dv_{*}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v_{*})}\,[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv_{*}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}.\end{aligned}

(2.4)

From the law of momentum conservation, for
\theta\geq 0
,

w^{2l}(v)\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v_{*})}\leq Cw^{2l}(|v|+|v_{*}|)\leq Cw^{2l}(|v^{\prime}|+|v_{*}^{\prime}|).

(2.5)

Since

v^{\prime}=v-\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega,\qquad v^{\prime}_{*}=v_{*}+\frac{2m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega.

The Jacobian matrix ‌
\mathbf{J}
for the change of variables for
(v,v_{*})\rightarrow(v^{\prime},v_{*}^{\prime})
is the following
2\times 2
block matrix, where each block is a
3\times 3
matrix:

\mathbf{J}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial v^{\prime}}{\partial v}&\frac{\partial v^{\prime}_{*}}{\partial v}\\
\frac{\partial v^{\prime}}{\partial v_{*}}&\frac{\partial v^{\prime}_{*}}{\partial v_{*}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{I}-\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\omega\omega^{\rm{T}}&\frac{2m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\omega\omega^{\rm{T}}\\
\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\omega\omega^{\rm{T}}&\mathbf{I}-\frac{2m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\omega\omega^{\rm{T}}\end{pmatrix},

where
\mathbf{I}
is a
3\times 3
identity matrix, and
\omega=(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3})^{\rm{T}}
is a column vector. According to the linear transformation of the determinant:

\displaystyle\det\mathbf{J}=

\displaystyle\left|\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathbf{I}&\mathbf{I}\\
\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\omega\omega^{\rm{T}}&\mathbf{I}-\frac{2m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\omega\omega^{\rm{T}}\end{array}\right|=\left|\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathbf{I}&0\\
\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\omega\omega^{\rm{T}}&\mathbf{I}-2\omega\omega^{\rm{T}}\end{array}\right|.

Therefore

\displaystyle\det\mathbf{J}=

\displaystyle\det(\mathbf{I}-2\omega\omega^{\rm{T}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\begin{array}[]{ccc}1-2\omega_{1}^{2}&-2\omega_{1}\omega_{2}&-2\omega_{1}\omega_{3}\\
-2\omega_{2}\omega_{1}&1-2\omega_{2}\omega_{2}&-2\omega_{2}\omega_{3}\\
-2\omega_{3}\omega_{1}&-2\omega_{3}\omega_{2}&1-2\omega_{3}\omega_{3}\end{array}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-2\omega_{1}^{2})\big[(1-2\omega_{2}^{2})(1-2\omega_{3}^{2})-4\omega_{2}^{2}\omega_{3}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle+2\omega_{1}\omega_{2}\big[-2\omega_{1}\omega_{2}(1-2\omega^{2}_{3})-4\omega_{1}\omega_{2}\omega^{2}_{3}\big]

\displaystyle-2\omega_{1}\omega_{3}\big[4\omega_{1}\omega^{2}_{2}\omega_{3}+2\omega_{1}\omega_{3}(1-2\omega_{2}^{2})\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-2\omega^{2}_{1}-2\omega^{2}_{2}-2\omega^{2}_{3},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-1,

which implies

dv_{*}^{\prime}dv^{\prime}=|\det\mathbf{J}|dv_{*}dv=dv_{*}dv.

(2.6)

Additionally, it is straightforward to verify

v^{\prime}-v^{\prime}_{*}=v-v_{*}-2[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega.

As the picture below shows

Figure 4.
|v-v_{*}|=|(v-v_{*})-2\big[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega\big]\omega|

This is because any point on the perpendicular bisector is equidistant from the two endpoints of the line segment, so we obtain

|v-v_{*}|=|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|.

(2.7)

Then, the first line in
(
2.4
)
is bounded by

\displaystyle\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(|v^{\prime}|+|v_{*}^{\prime}|)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}\,\big[f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})\big]^{2}dv_{*}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\lesssim\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(|v^{\prime}|)|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}\,\big[f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})\big]^{2}dv_{*}^{\prime}\,dv^{\prime}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\lesssim\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}\,dv_{*}^{\prime}\,w^{2l}(|v^{\prime}|)\,\big[f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})\big]^{2}\,dv^{\prime}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\lesssim\,\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}\big|_{\nu},

and the second line in
(
2.4
)
is bounded by

\displaystyle\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v_{*})}\,[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv_{*}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\lesssim\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v_{*})}dv_{*}\,w^{2l}(v)\,[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\lesssim\,\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}.

Next, we consider the second term in (
2.3
), which is bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} C&\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}[f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})]^{2}dv_{*}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv_{*}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}.\end{aligned}

(2.8)

By a change of variables
(v,v_{*})\rightarrow(v^{\prime},v_{*}^{\prime})
, utilizing (
2.6
) and (
2.7
), the first line in (
2.8
) is bounded by

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(|v^{\prime}|+|v_{*}^{\prime}|)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}[f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})]^{2}dv_{*}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}dv_{*}^{\prime}\,w^{2l}(|v_{*}^{\prime}|)\,[f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})]^{2}\,dv^{\prime}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}\big|_{\nu},

and the second line in (
2.8
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}w^{2l}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\,[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv_{*}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\lesssim\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}dv_{*}\,w^{2l}(v)\,[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}\,dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\lesssim\,\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}.

Therefore, (
2.3
) is estimated and together with (
2.2
), this lemma thus follows.
∎

For
m>0
, we define

\begin{split}&K_{1,r}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu^{\alpha}(v)\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}f^{\beta}(v_{*})d\omega dv_{*},\\
&K_{2,r}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\Big[\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}f^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 241.84842pt+\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\,f^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\Big]d\omega dv_{*}.\end{split}

(2.9)

To replace ‌
\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}
with ‌
\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|\leq m}
in ‌(
2.9
), the resulting expression defines ‌
K_{1,z}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}
and ‌
K_{2,z}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}
, for
i=1,2
, satisfying

\begin{split}K_{i}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=K_{i,r}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}+K_{i,z}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f},\qquad K_{z}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=K_{1,z}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}+K_{2,z}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}\\
K_{r}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=K_{1,r}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}+K_{2,r}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f},\qquad K^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}=K_{r}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}+K_{z}^{\alpha}\mathbf{f}\end{split}

Furthermore‌, by choosing a smooth cutoff function
\chi_{r}
,

\chi(r)\equiv 1\text{ for }r\geq 2\varepsilon;\quad\chi(r)\equiv 0\text{ for }r\leq\varepsilon

(2.10)

we define

\begin{split}&K_{1,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\chi(|v-v_{*}|)\mu^{\alpha}(v)\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}f^{\beta}(v_{*})d\omega dv_{*},\\
&K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\chi(|v-v_{*}|)\Big[\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}f^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 258.92024pt+\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\,f^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\Big]d\omega dv_{*}.\end{split}

(2.11)

and

\begin{split}&K_{1,z}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|\leq m}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\chi(|v-v_{*}|)\mu^{\alpha}(v)\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}f^{\beta}(v_{*})d\omega dv_{*},\\
&K_{2,z}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}}}\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|\leq m}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\chi(|v-v_{*}|)\Big[\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}f^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 258.92024pt+\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\,f^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})\Big]d\omega dv_{*}.\end{split}

(2.12)

Replace ‌
\chi(|v-v_{*}|)
with ‌
\big[1-\chi(|v-v_{*}|)\big]
in ‌(
2.11
) and (
2.12
), the resulting expression defines ‌
K_{1,r}^{\alpha,1-\chi},\,K_{2,r}^{\alpha,1-\chi},\,K_{1,z}^{\alpha,1-\chi}
and ‌
K_{2,z}^{\alpha,1-\chi}
. It can be easily verified‌ that

\begin{split}K^{\alpha}=\big\{K^{\alpha}_{1}+K^{\alpha}_{2}\big\}=&\big\{K_{1,r}^{\alpha,\chi}+K_{1,z}^{\alpha,\chi}+K_{1,r}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{1,z}^{\alpha,1-\chi}\big\}\\
&+\big\{K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}+K_{2,z}^{\alpha,\chi}+K_{2,r}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{2,z}^{\alpha,1-\chi}\big\}\end{split}

(2.13)

Furthermore, we define

\mathbf{K}_{s}=(K^{A}_{s},K^{B}_{s})^{T},\hskip 17.07182pt\mathbf{K}_{c}=(K^{A}_{c},K^{B}_{c})^{T}

by

\begin{split}&K^{\alpha}_{s}=K_{1,r}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{1,z}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{2,r}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{2,z}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{1,r}^{\alpha,\chi}+K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\\
&K^{\alpha}_{c}=K_{1,z}^{\alpha,\chi}+K_{2,z}^{\alpha,\chi}\end{split}

(2.14)

and define

\begin{split}K^{\alpha,1-\chi}_{1,s}&=K_{1,r}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{1,z}^{\alpha,1-\chi}\\
K^{\alpha,1-\chi}_{2,s}&=K_{2,r}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{2,z}^{\alpha,1-\chi}\\
K^{\alpha}_{1,s}&=K_{1,r}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{1,z}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{1,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\\
K^{\alpha}_{2,s}&=K_{2,r}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{2,z}^{\alpha,1-\chi}+K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\end{split}

(2.15)

By combining (
2.13
) and (
2.14
), we can verify that
\mathbf{K}=\mathbf{K}_{c}+\mathbf{K}_{s}
.

Moreover, in the following lemma, we will prove that
\mathbf{K}_{s}
is a “small part” and
\mathbf{K}_{c}
is a “compact part”.

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
l\geq 0
,
\mathbf{f}_{i},(i=1,2)
be two vector functions. The operator
\mathbf{K}
defined in (
1.25
) and (
1.26
) is split as

\mathbf{K}=\mathbf{K}_{c}+\mathbf{K}_{s},

then for any small
1>\eta>0
,

\big|\big\langle w^{2l}\mathbf{K}_{s}\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\big|\leq\eta|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}|_{\nu}|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}|_{\nu}.

(2.16)

Moreover,
\mathbf{K}_{c}
is a compact operator in
L^{2}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
with respect to
|\cdot|_{\nu}
.

Proof.

By (
2.14
) and (
2.15
), we decompose
K_{s}^{\alpha}
as follows:

K_{s}^{\alpha}=K_{1,s}^{\alpha}+K_{2,s}^{\alpha}.

(2.17)

We first consider the part of
K_{1,s}^{\alpha}
, which can be written as

K_{1,s}^{\alpha}=K^{\alpha,1-\chi}_{1,s}+K_{1,r}^{\alpha,\chi}.

Since the angular part of the collision kernel
|b^{\alpha\beta}|\leq C_{b}|\cos\theta|
,
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{1,s}^{\alpha,1-\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
is bounded by

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\left(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v)}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}w^{2l}(v)(1-\chi)[f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*})]^{2}\,dv_{*}dv\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt\times\left(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v)}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}w^{2l}(v)(1-\chi)[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}\,dv_{*}dv\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}(1-\chi)dv\,w^{2l+\frac{\gamma}{2}}(v_{*})[\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})]^{\frac{1}{4}}[f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*})]^{2}dv_{*}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}(1-\chi)dv_{*}\,w^{2l+\frac{\gamma}{2}}(v)[\mu^{\alpha}(v)]^{\frac{1}{4}}[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}\varepsilon^{3+\gamma}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w^{2l+\frac{\gamma}{2}}(v_{*})[f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*})]^{2}dv_{*}\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w^{2l+\frac{\gamma}{2}}(v)[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{3+\gamma}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}\big|_{\nu}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}.

(2.18)

Using the notation (
1.19
),
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{1,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
is bounded by

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{\{|v|+|u|\geq m\}}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v)}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}|v-u|^{\gamma}w^{2l}\chi(|v-v_{*}|)f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*})\,dv_{*}dv

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}[\mu^{s}(m)]^{\frac{1}{8}}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}[\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})]^{\frac{1}{4}}[\mu^{\alpha}(v)]^{\frac{1}{4}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*})|\,dv_{*}dv

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}[\mu^{s}(m)]^{\frac{1}{8}}\left(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}[\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})]^{\frac{1}{4}}[\mu^{\alpha}(v)]^{\frac{1}{4}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}[f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*})]^{2}\,dv_{*}dv\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 96.73918pt\times\left(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}[\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})]^{\frac{1}{4}}[\mu^{\alpha}(v)]^{\frac{1}{4}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}\,dv_{*}dv\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C[\mu^{s}(m)]^{\frac{1}{8}}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}\big|_{\nu}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}.

(2.19)

The proof for
K^{\alpha}_{2}
part is more complicated than that of
K^{\alpha}_{1}
.
We write
K_{2,s}^{\alpha}
as

K_{2,s}^{\alpha}=K^{\alpha,1-\chi}_{2,s}+K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}.

The expression for
K^{\alpha}_{2}
in (
1.26
) and (
2.15
) can be simplified, by using the conservation of energy:
m^{\beta}|v_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}+m^{\alpha}|v^{\prime}|^{2}=m^{\beta}|v_{*}|^{2}+m^{\alpha}|v|^{2}
,

\begin{split}&K_{2,s}^{\alpha,1-\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1}=-\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}(1-\chi)\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\\
&\hskip 170.71652pt\times\Big(\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})+\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}\,f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})\Big)d\omega dv_{*}.\end{split}

‌Therefore,
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,s}^{\alpha,1-\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
is decomposed into two components based on
K_{2,s}^{\alpha}
. The part containing
f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})
is bounded by

\begin{split}C\sum_{\beta=A,B}&\left(\iiint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}w^{2l}(1-\chi)[f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})]^{2}d\omega dv_{*}dv\right)^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\left(\iiint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}w^{2l}(1-\chi)[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}d\omega dv_{*}dv\right)^{1/2}\end{split}

(2.20)

Although
m^{A}\neq m^{B}
, a direct computation shows that
|v_{*}-v|=|v_{*}^{\prime}-v^{\prime}|
still holds. Since
|v_{*}-v|\leq 2\varepsilon
, it follows that

\displaystyle|v_{*}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=\big|v^{\prime}-(v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime})\big|\geq|v^{\prime}|-|v-v_{*}|\geq|v^{\prime}|-2\varepsilon,

\displaystyle|v_{*}|

\displaystyle=\big|v_{*}^{\prime}+\frac{2m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}[(v_{*}-v)\cdot\omega]\omega\big|\geq|v_{*}^{\prime}|-2|v_{*}-v|\geq|v^{\prime}|-6\varepsilon.

Therefore, for
0<\varepsilon<1
,

\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\leq C\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v^{\prime})}

By (
2.6
), the square of first line in (
2.20
) is bounded by

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iiint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}w^{2l}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\big[1-\chi(|v_{*}-v|)\big][f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})]^{2}\,dv_{*}\,dv\,d\omega

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}w^{2l}(v)|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\big[1-\chi(|v_{*}^{\prime}-v^{\prime}|)\big][f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})]^{2}\,dv_{*}\,dv

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}[\mu^{\beta}(v^{\prime})]^{\frac{1}{4}}[\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})]^{\frac{1}{4}}w^{2l}(v^{\prime})|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\big[1-\chi(|v_{*}^{\prime}-v^{\prime}|)\big][f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})]^{2}\,dv_{*}\,dv

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|\leq 2\varepsilon}[\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})]^{\frac{1}{4}}|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\,dv_{*}^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\mu^{\beta}(v^{\prime})]^{\frac{1}{4}}w^{2l}(v^{\prime})[f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})]^{2}\,dv^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{3+\gamma}|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}|_{\nu}^{2}

We note that

\displaystyle|v_{*}|

\displaystyle=|v-(v-v_{*})|\geq|v|-|v-v_{*}|\geq|v|-2\varepsilon,

\displaystyle|v_{*}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=|v_{*}-\frac{2m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}[(v_{*}-v)\cdot\omega]\omega|\geq|v_{*}|-2|v_{*}-v|\geq|v|-6\varepsilon,

so it holds that
\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}\leq C\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v)}
.

Hence, the square of second line in (
2.20
) is
bounded by

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v)}|v-u|^{\gamma}\big[1-\chi(|v_{*}-v|)\big]\,dv_{*}\,[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}\,dv

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq 2\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\,dv_{*}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v)}[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}\,dv

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{3+\gamma}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}^{2}

So, (
2.20
) is bounded by
C\varepsilon^{3+\gamma}|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}|_{\nu}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}
.

Therefore, we have obtained the estimates of the first part in
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,s}^{\alpha,1-\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
.

Similarly, the part of containing
f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})
in
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,s}^{\alpha,1-\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
is bounded by

\begin{split}C\sum_{\beta=A,B}&\left(\iiint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}w^{2l}(1-\chi)[f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})]^{2}d\omega dv_{*}dv\right)^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\left(\iiint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}w^{2l}(1-\chi)[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}d\omega dv_{*}dv\right)^{1/2}\end{split}

(2.21)

Since
|v_{*}-v|\leq 2\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle|v_{*}|

\displaystyle=|v_{*}^{\prime}-\frac{2m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega|\geq|v_{*}^{\prime}|-2|v_{*}-v|\geq|v_{*}^{\prime}|-4\varepsilon,

\displaystyle|v^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=|v-\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega|\geq|v|-2|v_{*}-v|\geq|v|-4\varepsilon

For
\varepsilon<1
, we have

\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\leq C\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})},\quad\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\leq C\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v)}.

So, the square of the first line in (
2.21
) is bounded by

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iiint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}w^{2l}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\big[1-\chi(|v_{*}-v|)\big][f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})]^{2}\,dv_{*}\,dv\,d\omega

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}w^{2l}(v)|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\big[1-\chi(|v_{*}^{\prime}-v^{\prime}|)\big][f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})]^{2}\,dv_{*}^{\prime}\,dv^{\prime}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}[\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})]^{\frac{1}{4}}[\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})]^{\frac{1}{4}}w^{2l}(v_{*}^{\prime})|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\big[1-\chi(|v_{*}^{\prime}-v^{\prime}|)\big][f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})]^{2}\,dv_{*}^{\prime}\,dv^{\prime}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|\leq 2\varepsilon}[\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})]^{\frac{1}{4}}|v^{\prime}-v_{*}^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\,dv^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\mu^{\beta}(v^{\prime}_{*})]^{\frac{1}{4}}w^{2l}(v^{\prime}_{*})[f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})]^{2}\,dv_{*}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq C\varepsilon^{3+\gamma}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}\big|_{\nu}^{2}

The square of the second line in (
2.21
) is bounded by

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v)}|v-u|^{\gamma}\big[1-\chi(|v_{*}-v|)\big]\,dv_{*}\,[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}\,dv

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq 2\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\,dv_{*}\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v)}[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}\,dv

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{3+\gamma}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}^{2}

So, (
2.21
) is also bounded by
C\varepsilon^{3+\gamma}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}\big|_{\nu}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}
.

‌In conclusion‌, we have

\left\langle w^{2l}\mathbf{K}_{2,s}^{1-\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\sum_{\alpha=A,B}\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,s}^{\alpha,1-\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\leq C\varepsilon^{3+\gamma}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}\big|_{\nu}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}

(2.22)

We now analyze the rest part

\begin{split}&-K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1}=\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*})}\chi(|v-v_{*}^{\prime}|)\\
&\hskip 159.3356pt\times\Big(\sqrt{\mu^{\alpha}(v^{\prime})}f^{\beta}_{1}(v_{*}^{\prime})+\sqrt{\mu^{\beta}(v_{*}^{\prime})}\,f^{\alpha}_{1}(v^{\prime})\Big)d\omega dv_{*}.\end{split}

(2.23)

Since its truncation function no longer supplies a small quantity, we perform a change of variable

\displaystyle u=v_{*}-v,\quad u_{\parallel}=(u\cdot\omega)\omega,\quad u_{\perp}=u-(u\cdot\omega)\omega,

\displaystyle\zeta_{\parallel}+\zeta_{\perp}=v+\frac{m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel},\quad\zeta_{\parallel}\parallel u_{\parallel},\quad\zeta_{\perp}\parallel u_{\perp},

(2.24)

where
u\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
,
u_{\parallel}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
u_{\perp}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and

|u_{\parallel}|^{2}(|u_{\parallel}|^{-1}d|u_{\parallel}|)d\omega=du_{\parallel},\qquad du={du}^{+}+{du}^{-}=2(|u_{\parallel}|^{-1}d|u_{\parallel}|)du_{\perp}.

Here,
(|u_{\parallel}|^{-1}d|u_{\parallel}|)
can be understood as “
dr
” in spherical coordinate transformation. Since the direction of the coordinate variable
(|u_{\parallel}|^{-1}d|u_{\parallel}|)
is only one direction of
\omega
or
-\omega
. So, it yields

{du}^{+}=\mathbf{1}_{\{u\cdot\omega\geq 0\}}du=(|u_{\parallel}|^{-1}d|u_{\parallel}|)du_{\perp},\hskip 14.22636pt{du}^{-}=\mathbf{1}_{\{u\cdot\omega<0\}}du=(|u_{\parallel}|^{-1}d|u_{\parallel}|)du_{\perp}.

Therefore,

dud\omega=2|u_{\parallel}|^{-2}du_{\perp}du_{\parallel}.

(2.25)

Then
(-1)\times
(
2.23
) can be expressed by

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|u|^{\gamma}[\mu^{\beta}(u+v)]^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi(|u|)\big[\mu^{\beta}(v+u_{\perp}+\frac{m^{\beta}-m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 156.49014pt\times f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)\,du\,d\omega\Bigg)

(2.26)

\displaystyle+\int_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|u|^{\gamma}[\mu^{\beta}(u+v)]^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi(|u|)\big[\mu^{\alpha}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 28.45274pt(\alpha\neq\beta)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times f^{\beta}_{1}(v+u_{\perp}+\frac{m^{\beta}-m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)\,du\,d\omega

(2.27)

\displaystyle+\int_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|u|^{\gamma}[\mu^{\alpha}(u+v)]^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi(|u|)\big[\mu^{\alpha}(v+u_{\parallel})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+u_{\perp})b^{\alpha\alpha}(\theta)\,du\,d\omega

(2.28)

Here, (
2.26
) is the integral for “Typical part”. (
2.27
)
+
(
2.28
) is the integral for “Hybrid part”, where (
2.27
) exhibit exponential decay and (
2.28
) is the degenerate part.

Notice that

|u_{\parallel}|=|u\cos\theta|=\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\,|\cos\theta|

Furthermore, by (
2.24
), it holds that

\displaystyle u+v

\displaystyle=u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}+\zeta_{\parallel}+\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel},

(2.29)

\displaystyle v+u_{\perp}

\displaystyle+\frac{m^{\beta}-m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel}=u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}+\zeta_{\parallel}-\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel},

where

\zeta_{\perp}=v_{\perp},\hskip 14.22636pt\zeta_{\parallel}=v_{\parallel}+\frac{m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel}.

(2.30)

Therefore, the exponent in (
2.26
) is
-\frac{1}{2}\Big(|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}+|\zeta_{\parallel}|^{2}+(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}\Big)
.

Hence, (
2.26
) can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}\big[|\zeta_{\parallel}|^{2}+(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}\big]}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\chi\big(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\big)b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)\,du_{\perp}du_{\parallel}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}\big[|\zeta_{\parallel}|^{2}+(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}\big]}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\chi\big(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\big)\frac{b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)}{|\cos\theta|}\,du_{\perp}du_{\parallel}.

(2.31)

We introduce the integral kernel:

\displaystyle k_{\alpha\beta}^{(1),r}(v,u_{\parallel})

\displaystyle=:\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}\big[|\zeta_{\parallel}|^{2}+(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}\big]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\times\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\chi\big(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\big)\frac{b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)}{|\cos\theta|}\,du_{\perp}.

(2.32)

Then (
2.26
) can be further written as

\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}k_{\alpha\beta}^{(1),r}(v,u_{\parallel})\,f^{\alpha}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})\,du_{\parallel}

Next we turn to the other part of (
2.23
). Note that in the part of (
2.27
),
\mu^{\alpha}
and
\mu^{\beta}
may be different. To clearly characterize their exponential decay, we apply the following change of variable:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\xi_{\parallel}+\xi_{\perp}&=\frac{\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}+\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}v+\big(\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\big)u_{\parallel}+\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}u_{\perp},\end{split}

(2.33)

where
\xi_{\parallel}\parallel u_{\parallel},\,\,\xi_{\perp}\parallel u_{\perp}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36958pt=\xi_{\parallel}+\xi_{\perp}-\Big[\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}-\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}}{2}v+\big(\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}-\frac{\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\big)u_{\parallel}+\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}u_{\perp}\Big],

\displaystyle\sqrt{m^{\beta}}(v+u_{\parallel}+u_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36958pt=\xi_{\parallel}+\xi_{\perp}+\Big[\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}-\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}}{2}v+\big(\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}-\frac{\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\big)u_{\parallel}+\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}u_{\perp}\Big]

To keep the notation concise, we make the following change of variable

\displaystyle\begin{split}\eta_{\parallel}&=\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}-\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}}{2}v_{\parallel}+\big(\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}-\frac{\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\big)u_{\parallel}\\
\eta_{\perp}&=\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}-\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}}{2}v_{\perp}+\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}u_{\perp},\end{split}

(2.34)

where
v=v_{\parallel}+v_{\perp},v_{\parallel}\parallel u_{\parallel},v_{\perp}\parallel u_{\perp}
.
Then, we deduce that the exponent in (
2.27
) is

-\frac{1}{4}\Big(|\xi_{\parallel}|^{2}+|\xi_{\perp}|^{2}+|\eta_{\parallel}|^{2}+|\eta_{\perp}|^{2}\Big).

Therefore, (
2.27
) can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(|\xi_{\parallel}|^{2}+|\eta_{\parallel}|^{2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)\chi\big(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times e^{-\frac{1}{4}(|\xi_{\perp}|^{2}+|\eta_{\perp}|^{2})}\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}f^{\beta}_{1}(v+u_{\perp}+\frac{m^{\beta}-m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})\,du_{\perp}du_{\parallel}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(|\xi_{\parallel}|^{2}+|\eta_{\parallel}|^{2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}\frac{b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)}{|\cos\theta|}\chi\big(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times e^{-\frac{1}{4}(|\xi_{\perp}|^{2}+|\eta_{\perp}|^{2})}\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}f^{\beta}_{1}(v+u_{\perp}+\frac{m^{\beta}-m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})\,du_{\perp}du_{\parallel}.

(2.35)

We introduce the integral kernel
(\alpha\neq\beta)
:

\displaystyle k_{\alpha\beta}^{(2),r}(v,u_{\perp},u_{\parallel})

\displaystyle=:\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(|\xi_{\parallel}|^{2}+|\eta_{\parallel}|^{2})}\chi\big(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\big)\mathbf{1}_{|v|+|v_{*}|>m}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times e^{-\frac{1}{4}(|\xi_{\perp}|^{2}+|\eta_{\perp}|^{2})}\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\frac{b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)}{|\cos\theta|}.

(2.36)

Then (
2.27
) can be further written as

\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{\alpha\beta}^{(2),r}(v,u_{\perp},u_{\parallel})\,f^{\beta}(v+u_{\perp}+\frac{m^{\beta}-m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})\,du_{\perp}\,du_{\parallel}.

If
m^{\alpha}>m^{\beta}
, we perform a change of variable:

-u\rightarrow u
,
-v\rightarrow v
.
Without loss of generality, we assume
m^{\beta}\geq m^{\alpha}
, and we will divide the proof of (
2.16
) for
\mathbf{K}_{2,r}^{\chi}
part into two steps.

\mathbf{Step1}
. We consider the decay estimate for the part (
2.31
) in
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
.

Noting that after performing the variable substitution
(v_{*}-v)\rightarrow u
in (
2.24
), the region of integration becomes

\big(|v|+|v_{*}|\geq m\big)=\big(|v|+|u+v|\geq m\big)\subseteq\big(|v|+|u|\geq\frac{m}{2}\big)

Therefore, we partition the integration domain as follows:

\begin{split}\big(|v|+|v_{*}|\geq m\big)\subseteq\big(|v|+|u|\geq\frac{m}{2}\big)\subseteq\Big[(|v|\geq\frac{m}{10})\cup(|u|\geq\frac{2m}{5})\Big]\end{split}

We further split

\begin{split}\big(|v|\geq\frac{m}{10}\big)=&\Biggl\{\Big[(|v|\geq\frac{m}{10})\cap(|u_{\parallel}|\geq\frac{|v|}{8})\Big]\bigcup\Big[(|v|\geq\frac{m}{10})\cap(|u_{\perp}|\geq\frac{|v|}{8})\Big]\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt\bigcup\Big[(|v|\geq\frac{m}{10})\cap(|v_{\parallel}|\geq\frac{|v|}{2})\cap(|u_{\parallel}|\leq\frac{|v|}{8})\cap(|u_{\perp}|\leq\frac{|v|}{8})\Big]\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt\bigcup\Big[(|v|\geq\frac{m}{10})\cap(|v_{\perp}|\geq\frac{|v|}{2})\cap(|u_{\parallel}|\leq\frac{|v|}{8})\cap(|u_{\parallel}|\leq\frac{|v|}{8})\Big]\Biggr\}\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\Big[(|u|\geq\frac{2m}{5})\cap(|v|\leq\frac{m}{10})\Big]=&\Biggl\{\Big[(|u|\geq\frac{2m}{5})\cap(|v|\leq\frac{m}{10})\cap(|u_{\parallel}|\geq\frac{|u|}{2})\Big]\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt\bigcup\Big[(|u|\geq\frac{2m}{5})\cap(|v|\leq\frac{m}{10})\cap(|u_{\perp}|\geq\frac{|u|}{2})\Big]\Biggr\}\end{split}

Thus, the estimate of
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
can be divided into the following four cases for discussion.

Case A1.
D_{A1}=\Big[(|v|\geq\frac{m}{10})\cap(|u_{\parallel}|\geq\frac{|v|}{8})\Big]\bigcup\Big[(|u|\geq\frac{2m}{5})\cap(|v|\leq\frac{m}{10})\cap(|u_{\parallel}|\geq\frac{|u|}{2})\Big]
.

Since
\chi=\chi_{\varepsilon}
vanishes near the origin and

|B(\theta)|\leq C|\cos\theta|
, for the chosen
\varepsilon>0
and any given
m>0
,

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\chi\big(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\big)\frac{b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)}{|\cos\theta|}\,du_{\perp}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq C_{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}du_{\perp}<\infty,\end{split}

Notice that in both case of A1,
|u_{\parallel}|\geq\frac{|v|}{8}\geq\frac{m}{80}>1
, we have

Ce^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{16}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}\leq w^{\gamma-1}(v).

(2.37)

Then, the part of (
2.31
) in
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\big[|\zeta_{\parallel}|^{2}+(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}\big]}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})w^{2l}(v)f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\chi\big(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\big)\frac{b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)}{|\cos\theta|}\mathbf{1}_{D_{A1}}\,du_{\perp}du_{\parallel}dv

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})w^{\gamma}(v)w^{2l}(v)f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)du_{\parallel}dv

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}[f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})]^{2}w^{2l+\gamma}(v)du_{\parallel}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

(2.38)

\displaystyle\hskip 125.19194pt\times\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}du_{\parallel}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w^{2l+\gamma}(v)[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

Utilizing the inequality

w^{2l+\gamma}(v)\leq Cw^{2l+\gamma}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})w^{2l+\gamma}(\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel}),

(2.39)

and (
2.37
),
the last inequality in (
2.38
) is bounded by

\begin{split}&\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}w^{-2\gamma}(u_{\parallel})w^{2l+\gamma}(\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})du_{\parallel}\\
&\hskip 76.82234pt\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})w^{2l+\gamma}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})dv\Bigg)^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}du_{\parallel}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w^{2l+\gamma}(v)[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}\\
&\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}\big|_{\nu}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}.\end{split}

Case A2.
D_{A2}=\Big[(|v|\geq\frac{m}{10})\cap(|u_{\perp}|\geq\frac{|v|}{8})\Big]\bigcup\Big[(|u|\geq\frac{2m}{5})\cap(|v|\leq\frac{m}{10})\cap(|u_{\perp}|\geq\frac{|u|}{2})\Big]
.

In both case of A2,
|u_{\perp}|\geq\frac{m}{80}>1
, it holds that

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\chi\big(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\big)\frac{b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)}{|\cos\theta|}\mathbf{1}_{D_{A2}}\,du_{\perp}\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{1+|v|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}|u_{\perp}|^{\gamma}\mathbf{1}_{D_{A2}}du_{\perp}\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}|u_{\perp}|^{\gamma}\mathbf{1}_{D_{A2}}du_{\perp}\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}w^{\gamma}(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}du_{\perp}\end{split}

(2.40)

Then, the part of (
2.31
) in
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}[|\zeta_{\parallel}|^{2}+(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}]}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})w^{2l}(v)f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)

\displaystyle\hskip 25.6073pt\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\chi\big(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\big)\frac{b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)}{|\cos\theta|}\mathbf{1}_{D_{A2}}\,du_{\perp}du_{\parallel}dv

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})w^{\gamma}(v)w^{2l}(v)f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)du_{\parallel}dv

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}[f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})]^{2}w^{2l+\gamma}(v)du_{\parallel}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

(2.41)

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89418pt\times\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}du_{\parallel}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w^{2l+\gamma}(v)[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

By (
2.39
), it can be concluded that the last inequality in (
2.41
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}w^{2l+\gamma}(\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})du_{\parallel}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})w^{2l+\gamma}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}du_{\parallel}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w^{2l+\gamma}(v)[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}\big|_{\nu}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}.

Case A3.
D_{A3}=\Big(|v|\geq\frac{m}{10}\Big)\cap\Big(|v_{\parallel}|\geq\frac{|v|}{2}\Big)\cap\Big(|u_{\parallel}|\leq\frac{|v|}{8}\Big)\cap\Big(|u_{\perp}|\leq\frac{|v|}{8}\Big)
.

From (
2.29
), we have

\zeta_{\parallel}=\frac{m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel}+v_{\parallel},\qquad\zeta_{\perp}=v_{\perp},

(2.42)

with the assumption
m^{\beta}>m^{\alpha}
, so that
|\zeta_{\parallel}|\geq\frac{|v|}{4}\geq\frac{m}{40}
. We deduce that

e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{2}|\zeta_{\parallel}|^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{m}w^{\gamma-1}(v).

(2.43)

Then, the part of (
2.31
) in
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\big[|\zeta_{\parallel}|^{2}+(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}\big]}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})w^{2l}(v)f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\chi\big(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\big)\frac{b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)}{|\cos\theta|}\mathbf{1}_{D_{A3}}\,du_{\perp}du_{\parallel}dv

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})w^{2l+\gamma}(v)f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)du_{\parallel}dv

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\Bigg(\iint_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}[f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})]^{2}w^{2l+\gamma}(v)du_{\parallel}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89418pt\times\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}du_{\parallel}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w^{2l+\gamma}(v)[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}\big|_{\nu}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}.

Case A4.
D_{A4}=\Big(|v|\geq\frac{m}{10}\Big)\cap\Big(|v_{\perp}|\geq\frac{|v|}{2}\Big)\cap\Big(|u_{\parallel}|\leq\frac{|v|}{8}\Big)\cap\Big(|u_{\perp}|\leq\frac{|v|}{8}\Big)
.

In both cases of A4, we have

|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|\geq\frac{|v|}{4}\geq\frac{m}{40}>1.

Then, we get

e^{-\frac{m^{\beta}}{8}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{m}w^{\gamma-1}(v).

(2.44)

The part of (
2.31
) in
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=A,B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\big[|\zeta_{\parallel}|^{2}+(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}\big]}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})w^{2l}(v)f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}|u_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}\left[|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\chi\left(\sqrt{|u_{\parallel}|^{2}+|u_{\perp}|^{2}}\right)\frac{b^{\alpha\beta}(\theta)}{|\cos\theta|}\mathbf{1}_{D_{A4}}\,du_{\perp}du_{\parallel}dv

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})w^{2l+\gamma}(v)f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)du_{\parallel}dv

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\Bigg(\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}[f^{\alpha}_{1}(v+\frac{2m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}u_{\parallel})]^{2}w^{2l+\gamma}(v)du_{\parallel}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 82.51282pt\times\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{|u_{\parallel}|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{m^{\alpha}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})^{2}|u_{\parallel}|^{2}}du_{\parallel}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w^{2l+\gamma}(v)[f^{\alpha}_{2}(v)]^{2}dv\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{m}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{1}\big|_{\nu}\big|w^{l}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|_{\nu}.

Hence, we have obtained the estimates for the part of (
2.31
) in
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
.

\mathbf{Step2}
. We consider the decay estimate for the part (
2.35
) in
\left\langle w^{2l}K_{2,r}^{\alpha,\chi}\mathbf{f}_{1},f^{\alpha}_{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}
.

By (
2.33
), it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\xi_{\parallel}&=\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}+\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}}{2}v_{\parallel}+(\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})u_{\parallel}\\
\xi_{\perp}&=\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}+\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}}{2}v_{\perp}+\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}u_{\perp},\end{split}

(2.45)

By direct calculation, it shows that
(\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}-\frac{\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}})>0
, and

\big(\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}-\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}}{2}\big):\big(\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}-\frac{\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\big)>\big(\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}+\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}}{2}\big):\big(\frac{\sqrt{m^{\beta}}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{m^{\alpha}}m^{\beta}}{m^{\alpha}+m^{\beta}}\big).

(2.46)

Then, (
2.34
) and (
2.45
) indicate that if we regard
